# **ChainTorrent MVP**

## Problem Statement

JavaScript dependency distribution runs through a single centralized registry that is an availability chokepoint, a mutable source of truth, and an intermediary between the people who publish packages and the people who consume them. ChainTorrent's MVP replaces that path with a content-addressed swarm, a canonical on-chain identity registry, and transferable access entitlements, so that the distribution and identity model is proven with monetization out of bounds.

**Adoption runs individual developer first, and everything else follows from that.** A developer at a terminal gains cross-project reuse of whatever their machine has already fetched, swarm retrieval on popular packages, and installs that survive a registry outage. Build platforms adopt next and for their own reasons: a service running the same few thousand popular installs continuously, on always-on machines with real bandwidth, holds the ideal cache as a by-product of its own economics, and seeding that cache costs almost nothing once it exists. That makes them natural superseeders rather than participants anyone has to recruit, and it is why no privileged bootstrap host is part of this plan. CI and enterprise adoption arrive as a consequence of that sequence rather than as targets to be won early.

The sequence matters to this workplan because it determines what the MVP measures. Latency is judged against interactive local installs rather than pipeline tolerance, since a developer notices a doubled install where a pipeline does not. Cache reuse is the headline adoption metric, because it is the benefit the adopting population actually experiences. Registry-outage survival remains true throughout and is simply not the lead, since it matters most to the population that adopts last.

The design is specified in `docs/research/cryptography.md` and bounded in `docs/research/MVP Scope.md`. Undetermined decisions are held in the To-Do list below.

The cryptographic construction is specified; its research record is `docs/research/cryptography-research-notebook.md`, and its statement is `docs/research/cryptography-critical-path.md`. A validation harness on both pairing curves produces the sizes, timings, and gas against the configured piece-group size and curve defaults, and nothing waits on it: every node reads the piece-group size from the deployment record and depends only on the code it calls.

## Objectives

Deliver an install path for JavaScript dependencies that survives registry outages, serves popular packages from a peer swarm, and exercises the identity and entitlement pipeline end to end.

Prove distribution and identity. Willingness to pay and seeder compensation are out of bounds and are held in the To-Do list; the transaction flow is proven at a nominal price on assets the project publishes, as a test instrument rather than a monetization step.

Every undetermined decision is carried in the To-Do list, which every workplan that stems from this one inherits.

## Expected Outcome

A developer installs dependencies and receives a working `node_modules` assembled from a local content-addressed store, a peer swarm, or the ingest source as fallback, with canonical identity resolved on-chain and an access entitlement minted to their wallet.

  * `[✅]`   `objective`
    * `[✅]`   Problem: the Solidity delivery verifier must accept exactly what the Rust reference verifier accepts, bit for bit, so its Foundry tests read mint and transfer statements with proofs the reference produced from committed vector files, one encoded record per line; a statement reaches the contract as its context values and its group elements, and a proof as its challenge and responses, with the three second-group first messages added where the verifier has only first-group arithmetic, so each record carries those and the uniforms the function drew, as Foundry's failure persistence records a run's inputs rather than its generator state; the parameter-set digest and the identity bytes are configured values, since no harness ticket computes a digest and the verifier takes the identity element as an input (CR-09 parity inputs; the dependency map's `harness-crypto/generate/solidity/proof_vectors` row and its conflict flag on the harness's configured parameter-set digest and identity bytes; the technical requirements' `<records>` entry for mint and transfer proofs; the Delivery proof and on-chain verifier milestone's scope and its exit, the Rust verifier and the deployed contract agreeing on every vector)
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the contexts arrive typed, in the delivery proof's forms, and the identity arrives admitted, paired with its setup scope; the function rebuilds no form and admits no identity
    * `[✅]`   Functional: each mint record is one mint delivery over the mint context and the admitted identity, its statement and proof the delivery's own, its draws the delivery's six uniforms in the order master, `u0`, `u1`, issue, recipient `x`, and recipient `y`
    * `[✅]`   Functional: each transfer record is a mint delivery over the mint context and the admitted identity followed by a transfer delivery over the transfer context, the mint delivery's parameter set and identity element, and its recipient as the holder; its statement and proof are the transfer delivery's own, and its draws the mint delivery's six uniforms followed by the transfer delivery's three, buyer `x`, buyer `y`, and the rerandomization
    * `[✅]`   Functional: every uniform has the length the pairing's scalar sampling bound declares; a record's draws are its deliveries' uniforms concatenated in draw order; the envelope coins and the proof nonces are drawn inside their adapters and are not recorded
    * `[✅]`   Functional: each record holds its context values as the canonical fields its transcript carries them in, its group elements in the pairing's precompile encodings, and its proof's challenge and responses as 32-byte big-endian encodings, with its second-group first messages' encodings concatenated in the first-group-only form and no bytes in the both-groups form
    * `[✅]`   Functional: a failed mint delivery and a failed transfer delivery are each returned unchanged in the error arm, the delivery's own error whole
    * `[✅]`   Functional: each record type's canonical description maps the record to its fields in member order, the context's form-typed fields at the kinds the delivery proof's forms declare, rebuilds the record from its fields read through the encoding family's canonical-field reader, each form-typed context field read as its form, carrying the reader's refusal unchanged, and states its member names in field order and nothing else about its members; `harness-crypto/generate/solidity` pairs each name with the description's field kind and the borrowed encoder's type name for that kind
    * `[✅]`   Non-functional: the function reads no clock and touches no filesystem; it names no curve, concrete, form, scope, count, identity, or context value; the counts arrive in its params, the typed contexts and the admitted identity in its payload, and every encoding is the selected pairing's

* `[✅]`   `harness-crypto/generate/solidity` **The Solidity concrete of the harness's generate family, one adapter over the borrowed pairing, hash-to-scalar adapter, encoder, randomness source, credential KEM, key agreement, and delivery proof with its chain forms: calls each function it owns, through the handle its constructor params carry, with each kind's admitted size, encodes every vector record into one `0x`-prefixed hex line, renders the constants library and the vector-record library through `render`, and writes every vector file under the cell directory the received declarations' names build inside the configured generated directory, and both libraries under their configured names in the generated directory outside every cell directory; the family's only module that touches the filesystem; adds, as its producers, the generate family's interface, the configured per-kind quantities type, the admitted per-kind size type, and the family's mock to the family's `factory` module `harness-crypto/generate/solidity/group_vectors` creates; draws its settings, names, file-name and directory types, vector-kind identifier, and declaration from `harness-crypto/generate/solidity/settings`**

  * `[✅]`   `objective`
    * `[✅]`   Problem: the Solidity pairing library and delivery verifier read their constants and test vectors from a committed generated directory written from the Rust reference, never by hand; it holds, outside every cell directory, a constants library the suite's contracts import and a vector-record library declaring per kind the record struct, the function decoding one line into it, and the constant holding the kind's file name, and one cell directory per emitted cell named by the received pairing declaration's concrete name and the borrowed encoder's declared name, holding one vector file per declared kind holding one encoded record per line, the cell's values record among them; every name, count, path, compiler version, and license is the configuration's, and the cell directory is built from the names the received pairing's and encoder's declarations carry, never chosen (CR-09 parity inputs; CR-10 and CR-11 on-chain vectors; the dependency map's `harness-crypto/generate/solidity` row and its conflict flags on committed vector files, declared and configured vector kinds, configured compiler version and license, the cell directory, and the generated directory; the technical requirements' file tree for `contracts/evm/generated`, its `<vector files>`, `<line>`, and `<records>` entries; the Delivery proof and on-chain verifier milestone's scope and its exit, each declared kind's vector file holding its configured count, its enumerated set, or one record per configured input, every line decoding through the vector-record library's function for that kind)
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the kinds the concrete emits, each with its sizing and whether it needs second-group arithmetic at the verifier, are the kinds of `SOLIDITY_GENERATE_DECLARATION`, which `harness-crypto/generate/solidity/settings` authors and the adapter reads as its `DECLARATION`
    * `[✅]`   Functional: a kind's names, its vector file name, struct name, decode function name, and path constant name, are the `SolidityVectorKindSettings` entry of the `SolidityGenerateSettings` that `harness-crypto/generate/solidity/settings` admits; a kind's configured quantities, a count, a term count, and a message length, each present only where the kind takes it, are the family's, carried in the factory's params beside the selection so every concrete admits them alike, the family's mock concrete included; a kind's sizing is never configured, being the declaration's, and the concrete is constructed with each declared kind's admitted size, which `harness-crypto/generate/factory` builds from the declared sizing and the configured quantities; a kind the declaration emits with no names or no admitted size is refused with the kind
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the kinds emitted are the declared kinds in declared order, a gated kind only where the pairing declares second-group arithmetic
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the constants library and the records library each carry their one configured file name and library name, used unchanged for every cell and written in the generated directory outside every cell directory; the cell directory is the generated directory joined with the name the received pairing's declaration supplies for its concrete and the name the borrowed encoder's declaration supplies, each admitted through `GeneratedFileName`, each refusal returned as the concrete's own error; the cell's values are the record `constants` returns, encoded and written as the cell-values kind's file in the cell directory beside that cell's vector files, its curve name the name the received pairing's declaration supplies
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the family's trait names, as its associated `Forms`, the chain forms a concrete's payload contexts are typed in, the Solidity concrete's being its delivery proof's; the payload's mint and transfer contexts arrive typed in those forms, each carrying its purpose, and its identities and proof identity arrive admitted, each paired with its setup scope; no concrete, the family's mock concrete included, rebuilds a form or admits an identity
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the constants library's entries are `constants`'s; each emitted kind's records are its function's, called with that kind's admitted size, the delivery-statement version, the declarations of the KEM, key agreement, and delivery proof, and the payload's typed contexts and admitted identities
    * `[✅]`   Functional: each record is encoded through the encoder under its kind's canonical description; a kind's file is one line per record in record order, each `0x`, the encoding's bytes as two lowercase hex digits each, and a line feed, with nothing after the last line feed
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the vector-record library declares, per emitted kind in declared order, the kind's struct with its description's member names, each member typed by the borrowed encoder's type name for the canonical field kind its description's `FIELDS` holds at that index, resolved before `render` is called, its decode function, and its path constant holding the kind's file name
    * `[✅]`   Functional: every value is computed and every library rendered before any file is written; the cell directory is created beneath the Foundry project root and the generated directory, then each kind's file is written in declared order, then the constants library and the vector-record library are written in the generated directory outside the cell directory, each under its configured file name; the paths written are returned in that order
    * `[✅]`   Functional: a refusal from a function the concrete owns, from an encoding, from a `render` constructor, or from the filesystem is returned unchanged in the error arm, a filesystem error with the path it concerned
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the concrete holds each function it owns as a handle typed by that function's `…Fn` type, received in its constructor params beside the adapters, and calls every one of them only through its handle, never as a named function; `harness-crypto/generate/factory` hands the real functions as the handles, and a test hands production-typed functions of its own, so each branch of `emit` runs with every callee substituted
    * `[✅]`   Non-functional: adding a further generate concrete is its own module with its own declaration and settings, a variant of `GenerateConcreteKind`, a variant of `EmitErrorReturn` carrying its error, and an arm in each family-owned function and in the factory; this node's types name no other concrete, and no module outside the family changes
    * `[✅]`   Non-functional: the concrete calls no factory and names no chain's forms, no path, no file, library, or symbol name, no count, no curve, no profile, no compiler version, no license, no scope, no identity, and no type name, each type name being the borrowed encoder's; it admits no context or identity and admits no encoder; it reads no clock; the filesystem is its outer edge

  * `[✅]`   `role`
    * `[✅]`   App module in the adapter role: the Solidity concrete of the harness's generate family, as the benchmark is over the clock; it adds the family's generic interface, per-kind settings type, per-kind quantities, and mock to `apps/harness-crypto/src/generate/factory`, the module `harness-crypto/generate/solidity/group_vectors` creates, the declaration types and the Solidity concrete's settings, names, and vector-kind identifier being `harness-crypto/generate/solidity/settings`'
    * `[✅]`   Owns the concrete, its constructor params, and its error; the settings, the names, the file-name and generated-directory types, the vector-kind identifier, and the declaration are `harness-crypto/generate/solidity/settings`'
    * `[✅]`   Encodes records, renders libraries, and writes files; does not compute a constant or a vector, which its functions do, and does not render text itself, which `render` does, each reached only through the handle its constructor params carry
    * `[✅]`   Does not construct an adapter, admit a configuration, admit a context or an identity, or select a concrete; `harness-crypto/run` and its consumers obtain each adapter through its factory or `chain/create_chain_forms`, `harness-crypto/config/admit_statements` admits each identity, `harness-crypto/run/admit_contexts` admits each context into the forms, `harness-crypto/run/kem_consumer` pairs each admitted identity with its setup scope, and `harness-crypto/generate/factory` admits the resolved chain forms and the borrowed encoder against the chain forms identifier and the encoding identifier this declaration requires, admits the family's per-kind configuration against this declaration, and constructs this concrete
    * `[✅]`   Does not author the generate family's factory function; `harness-crypto/generate/factory` does, after this concrete
    * `[✅]`   Does not carry a commit

  * `[✅]`   `module`
    * `[✅]`   Bounded context, in the family: the `factory` module of the `generate` module of `apps/harness-crypto`, holding `VectorCount` and `MsmTermCount` with their constructor params and refusals, which `harness-crypto/generate/solidity/group_vectors` declares there, `GenerateConcreteKind`, `VectorKindSizing`, `VectorKindDeclaration`, `GenerateDeclaration`, and `GENERATE_INTERFACE_VERSION`, which `harness-crypto/generate/solidity/settings` declares there, `IGenerateAdapter`, `VectorKindQuantities`, `VectorKindQuantity`, `VectorKindSize`, `VectorKindSettings`, `EmittedFile`, the emit params, payload, success, error, and return types, and the family's mock and builders

* `[✅]`   `contracts/evm/ParameterSets` **The suite's parameter-set records: each set's digest as its key, with its asset, suite identifier, suite-tagged material, identity scope, and liveness, registered live and retired only by the registry that deploys it, and read by the registry's sidecar-per-live-set rule; creates `contracts/evm`, its Foundry configuration, and its continuous-integration definition; authors its interface, its ABI description, and their interface test, mock, guard test, and unit test**

  * `[✅]`   `objective`
    * `[✅]`   Problem: the registry rejects a deployment record that lacks a sidecar root for a set live at registration, and retires a set only when no live entitlement remains under it, so the sets and their liveness are held as records the registry registers, retires, and reads; every contract node of the suite builds, formats, and tests under one toolchain, so the contract that creates the suite creates that toolchain (LC-09 and LC-01; the dependency map's `contracts/evm/ParameterSets` row and its Tools and dependencies decision; the technical requirements' `ParameterSet` record and Registry paragraph, `registerParameterSet` and `retireParameterSet`; the Delivery proof and on-chain verifier milestone's scope for `contracts/evm/ParameterSets`; the tech stack's contract toolchain row; the research notebook's retirement rule, a set retired only when the registry shows no live entitlement under it)
    * `[✅]`   Functional: a parameter-set record holds `id`, the parameter-set digest the record is keyed by, `asset`, `suiteId`, `material`, the suite-tagged public parameters as bytes, `identityScope`, a `uint8`, and `live`, a `bool`; the contract stores every member as it is given and interprets none, because suite-specific material and parameter sets are "interpreted only by the delivery verifier the adapter registry binds" (the technical requirements' On-chain state)
    * `[✅]`   Functional: `registerParameterSet` records a set and marks it live; `retireParameterSet` marks a recorded live set not live and keeps every other member of its record; a recorded id, live or retired, is never recorded again, so a retired set's record remains readable (LC-01: the suite represents "parameter sets and their live or retired state")
    * `[✅]`   Functional: only the address that deployed the contract may register or retire a set, the registry that deploys it; whether no live entitlement remains under a set is the registry's decision and is applied before it calls `retireParameterSet`
    * `[✅]`   Functional: `parameterSet(id)` returns the record of `id`, every member zero for an id never recorded, and `isParameterSetLive(id)` returns whether the record of `id` is live, the view the sidecar-per-live-set rule reads
    * `[✅]`   Functional: `contracts/evm/foundry.toml` configures the suite's toolchain and `.github/workflows/contracts.yml` proves it on Windows, macOS, and Linux with `forge build`, `forge fmt --check`, and `forge test`, each file authored complete
    * `[✅]`   Functional: `contracts/evm/abi/IParameterSets.json` declares the interface's functions, events, and errors, and `contracts/evm/src/IParameterSets.sol` realizes them in Solidity with the same names, order, and types
    * `[✅]`   Non-functional: the contract's state is the record mapping and its immutable registry address; it makes no external call and names no generated symbol; the interface is stated in one description and one realization, neither copied into another file

  * `[✅]`   `role`
    * `[✅]`   Contract suite contract: a Solidity contract of `contracts/evm` implementing the interface it authors, the records contract the registry is constructed to read, and the node that creates the suite's directory, toolchain, and workflow
    * `[✅]`   Owns its custom errors, its events, its `ParameterSet` struct, and its record mapping
    * `[✅]`   Has no provides file of its own: the public ABI is the provides
    * `[✅]`   Does not recompute or verify a digest, interpret the material or the identity scope, count entitlements, hold a per-asset list of sets, hold or verify a sidecar, resolve an adapter, deploy the registry, or hold a volume limit
    * `[✅]`   Does not create the generated directory, the deployments directory, or the ABI directory's other descriptions, and does not edit `.github/workflows/rust.yml`
    * `[✅]`   Has no integration test element: the contract has no collaborator and no integrated chain, so its public ABI is proven by the interface, guard, and unit blocks
    * `[✅]`   Does not carry a commit

  * `[✅]`   `module`
    * `[✅]`   Bounded context: `contracts/evm`, holding `foundry.toml`, `abi/IParameterSets.json`, `src/IParameterSets.sol`, `src/ParameterSets.interaction.spec.md`, `src/ParameterSets.sol`, `test/ParameterSets.interface.t.sol`, `test/ParameterSetsMock.sol`, `test/ParameterSets.guard.t.sol`, and `test/ParameterSets.t.sol`, and `.github/workflows/contracts.yml` at the repository root

    * `[✅]`   Construction: the contract stores `msg.sender` as the immutable registry address and records nothing; the deployer is the only address that may register or retire
    * `[✅]`   Register guards, in order and each a comparison before any state is written: condition the caller is not the registry address; outcome revert `CallerNotRegistry(caller)`; condition `id` is zero; outcome revert `ParameterSetIdUnset()`; condition `asset` is zero; outcome revert `AssetUnset()`; condition `suiteId` is zero; outcome revert `SuiteUnset()`; condition `material` has length zero; outcome revert `MaterialEmpty()`; condition the record of `id` has a nonzero `asset`, recorded live or retired; outcome revert `ParameterSetAlreadyRegistered(id)`
    * `[✅]`   Register success: the record of `id` is stored with `id`, `asset`, `suiteId`, `material`, `identityScope`, and `live` true, and `ParameterSetRegistered(id, asset, suiteId, identityScope)` is emitted
    * `[✅]`   Retire guards, in order: condition the caller is not the registry address; outcome revert `CallerNotRegistry(caller)`; condition the record of `id` has a zero `asset`; outcome revert `ParameterSetNotRegistered(id)`; condition the record's `live` is false; outcome revert `ParameterSetAlreadyRetired(id)`
    * `[✅]`   Retire success: the record's `live` becomes false, every other member is unchanged, and `ParameterSetRetired(id)` is emitted
    * `[✅]`   `parameterSet` returns the record of `id`, every member zero for an id never recorded; `isParameterSetLive` returns the record's `live`, false for an id never recorded; each reads state only
    * `[✅]`   Ordering: the caller decision precedes every other decision, the argument decisions precede the duplicate decision, and every decision precedes the state write; a revert leaves every record unchanged
    * `[✅]`   The contract calls no other contract; the decision whether no live entitlement remains under a set precedes a call to `retireParameterSet` and is made by the caller
    * `[✅]`   The contract's boundary is its public ABI, proven by the interface, guard, and unit blocks; the Rust integration elements do not apply to a contract

* `[✅]`   `domain/handshake_key_commitment` **Handshake key commitment, the 32 bytes of the Binding record's `bytes32` commitment to an identity's bound handshake public key together with the scheme identifier the key is bound under, admitted only when the commitment can name a bound key**

  * `[✅]`   `objective`
    * `[✅]`   Problem: the identity binding stores a commitment to the handshake public key and the scheme identifier the key is bound under, the key itself travelling in the binding transaction's calldata and in the anchored DID Document, so the stored width does not depend on the scheme; the chain family's attempt context carries the identity and this commitment, so a value naming no bound key must be refused before an attempt is built from it (the technical requirements' `Binding` schema, `handshakeKeyCommitment bytes32` and `scheme uint8`; the feature spec's identity binding criterion; the dependency map's `domain/handshake_key_commitment` row and its statement of the chain family's attempt context; the Delivery proof and on-chain verifier milestone's scope for `domain/handshake_key_commitment`)
    * `[✅]`   Functional: one type holds the commitment's 32 bytes and the `SignatureSchemeIdentifier` the key is bound under, each reachable only through a read accessor, and its only producer is a fallible constructor
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the constructor takes exactly 32 bytes, so the length is a fact of the params type and never a runtime check, and takes the scheme as the `SignatureSchemeIdentifier` `domain/signature_scheme_identifier` authors, which is admitted before this constructor runs, so the constructor checks no scheme
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the constructor refuses the all-zero commitment, which is what an unassigned `bytes32` storage slot reads as, so it names no key the Binding record holds
    * `[✅]`   Functional: every other 32-byte value is admitted with the scheme it is handed, and both are read back unchanged
    * `[✅]`   Non-functional: the module depends on the standard library and on the `signature_scheme_identifier` module alone; the `domain` crate's dependencies are unchanged

  * `[✅]`   `role`
    * `[✅]`   Domain: an owned value type in the protocol and domain ring, the bound key every attempt context and the identity binding name
    * `[✅]`   Does not compute, verify, or derive a commitment, and does not hold the handshake public key; the key travels in calldata and in the DID Document
    * `[✅]`   Does not assign, map, or validate a scheme identifier; `domain/signature_scheme_identifier` owns the type and its refusal
    * `[✅]`   Does not read the Binding record, compare a commitment with a key, or track whether a binding is current; the registry holds the record
    * `[✅]`   Does not decode a commitment from wire bytes of unknown length; the encoding family decodes a `bytes32` into the 32 bytes this constructor takes
    * `[✅]`   Does not create any other module of the `domain` crate
    * `[✅]`   Does not carry a commit

  * `[✅]`   `module`
    * `[✅]`   Bounded context: the `handshake_key_commitment` module of the `domain` crate, holding `HandshakeKeyCommitment`, its length, its constructor params, and its constructor's error and return types, and its public integration test under `crates/domain/tests`
    * `[✅]`   Adds the module to the existing `domain` crate at `crates/domain`; the crate's manifest is unchanged and its barrel gains this module's lines
    * `[✅]`   Outside: the handshake key, the commitment's computation and verification, the scheme's assignment, the Binding record, and the encoding of the commitment on the wire

  * `[✅]`   `deps`
    * `[✅]`   `domain/signature_scheme_identifier`, authored in `ChainTorrent MVP 3.md`, provides `SignatureSchemeIdentifier`, `build_signature_scheme_identifier`, and `SignatureSchemeIdentifierConstructorParamsOverrides` through `crate::signature_scheme_identifier::provides`; direction inward, a module of the same innermost crate; purpose, the scheme the commitment's key is bound under, held as the admitted type and built through its owner's builder
    * `[✅]`   `domain/secret` created the `domain` crate this module joins; this module imports nothing from `secret`, `deployment_identity`, `suite_identifier`, `parameter_set_identifier`, `group_index`, or `piece_geometry`
    * `[✅]`   The standard library: `u8`, arrays, and `Iterator::all`, through the prelude
    * `[✅]`   No external crate and no repository crate; `crates/domain/Cargo.toml` is unchanged; direction inward, `domain` is the innermost ring
    * `[✅]`   No reverse dependency; the chain family's attempt context is this module's first consumer

    * `[✅]`   Admitted: condition some byte of `params.bytes` is nonzero; decision the same check; dependency call none; outcome `Ok(HandshakeKeyCommitment { bytes, scheme })`, the array and the scheme moved from the params, the scheme unexamined because `SignatureSchemeIdentifier` is admitted by its own constructor

    * `[✅]`   Invariants: every `HandshakeKeyCommitment` holds 32 bytes, not all zero, and one admitted `SignatureSchemeIdentifier`; its only producer is `try_new`

    * `[✅]`   Own entry `an_admitted_commitment_reads_back_its_bytes_and_its_scheme`: condition `HandshakeKeyCommitment::try_new` called through the crate's public surface with nonzero bytes and a scheme admitted by `SignatureSchemeIdentifier::try_new`; outcome `Ok` whose `as_bytes()` is the bytes handed and whose `scheme().identifier()` is the identifier the scheme was admitted with; the input variation is bytes of 32 distinct values and a scheme differing from the builder's default, so a copy that reorders the bytes or supplies its own scheme fails

    * `[✅]`   `admission_refuses_an_address_with_a_non_hexadecimal_digit`: Contract: a forty-character body holding a non-hexadecimal digit → `Err(Address)`; Arrange: `entitlement_contract` `0x` followed by thirty-nine `a` digits and one `g`; Act: `admit_base_chain_settings`; Assert: `error` equals `Address { setting: "entitlement_contract".into(), text }` with that text
    * `[✅]`   `admission_admits_an_address_in_upper_case_digits`: Contract: hexadecimal digits of either case are admitted; Arrange: `entitlement_contract` `0x` followed by forty `A` digits; Act: `admit_base_chain_settings`; Assert: `entitlement_contract` equals twenty bytes each `0xaa`
    * `[✅]`   `admission_refuses_an_address_without_the_hexadecimal_prefix`: Contract: forty hexadecimal digits without `0x` → `Err(Address)`; Arrange: `entitlement_contract` forty `a` digits; Act: `admit_base_chain_settings`; Assert: `error` equals `Address { setting: "entitlement_contract".into(), text }` with that text
    * `[✅]`   `admission_refuses_a_registry_of_the_wrong_length`: Contract: the registry is admitted as an address is; Arrange: `registry` `0x1234`; Act: `admit_base_chain_settings`; Assert: `error` equals `Address { setting: "registry".into(), text: "0x1234".into() }`
    * `[✅]`   `admission_refuses_an_anchor_state_registry_of_the_wrong_length`: Contract: the anchor state registry is admitted as an address is; Arrange: `anchor_state_registry` `0x1234`; Act: `admit_base_chain_settings`; Assert: `error` equals `Address { setting: "anchor_state_registry".into(), text: "0x1234".into() }`
    * `[✅]`   `admission_refuses_a_batch_page_limit_of_zero`: Contract: a page limit of zero → `Err(BatchPageLimit { setting, text })`; Arrange: `batch_page_limit` `0`; Act: `admit_base_chain_settings`; Assert: `error` equals `BatchPageLimit { setting: "batch_page_limit".into(), text: "0".into() }`
    * `[✅]`   `admission_refuses_an_empty_batch_page_limit`: Contract: an empty page limit text → `Err(BatchPageLimit)`; Arrange: `batch_page_limit` empty; Act: `admit_base_chain_settings`; Assert: `error` equals `BatchPageLimit { setting: "batch_page_limit".into(), text: String::new() }`
    * `[✅]`   `admission_refuses_a_batch_page_limit_that_is_not_decimal`: Contract: a page limit text holding a non-digit → `Err(BatchPageLimit)`; Arrange: `batch_page_limit` `sixty`; Act: `admit_base_chain_settings`; Assert: `error` equals `BatchPageLimit { setting: "batch_page_limit".into(), text: "sixty".into() }`
    * `[✅]`   `admission_refuses_a_batch_page_limit_wider_than_64_bits`: Contract: a decimal value above the largest 64-bit unsigned value → `Err(BatchPageLimit)`; Arrange: `batch_page_limit` `18446744073709551616`; Act: `admit_base_chain_settings`; Assert: `error` equals `BatchPageLimit` carrying that text
    * `[✅]`   `admission_refuses_a_setting_the_declaration_does_not_state`: Contract: no statement of the setting's name → `Err(UndeclaredSetting)`; Arrange: a declaration from `build_chain_declaration` with `settings` at `&[]` and the empty document; Act: `admit_base_chain_settings`; Assert: `error` equals `UndeclaredSetting { setting: "endpoint".into() }`, the first setting read

* `[✅]`   `chain/base` **The Base concrete of the chain family, one adapter over `alloy`: Base's settlement tiers and reference age, the registry's three-state authorization view and its batch, a transaction's estimated fee composed from its execution gas and its L1 data fee, and the recovery of a delivered envelope from the settlement events and calldata; binds the forms `chain/create_chain_forms` resolves and authors none; authors the chain family's settlement and entitlement-state interfaces, its attempt context, selection, declared set, consumer surface, description of a fee, and mock, and the language-neutral ABI descriptions of the registry and of the entitlement interface**

  * `[✅]`   `objective`
    * `[✅]`   Problem: every attempt is gated on chain state read at a declared settlement tier, so the client needs one adapter that maps Base's heads onto the protocol's ordered tier vocabulary, evaluates an attempt context against the registry at one settlement reference, estimates a transaction's fee, and recovers a delivered envelope from chain history; the chain's network values are the adapter's configuration, reached through the family and never held by a consumer (LC-04; LC-06; CD-04; the Concrete Couplings Handoff's problem "the chain's network values" and its decision on the estimated fee; the dependency map's `chain/base` row and `chain/base ownership` list; the Delivery proof and on-chain verifier milestone's scope for `chain/base`; the technical requirements' contract-suite API surface, Database Schemas, and Chain family ownership; the product requirements' resolved position on Base and the contract suite as the on-chain half of the chain family; `docs/research/cryptography.md`, Settlement and the `AttemptContext` paragraph; `docs/research/MVP Scope.md`, Native Credentials and Per-Attempt Authorization; `families.md`, "A family's surface carries no concrete's model")
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the family's settlement interface maps a tier to the most recent reference that has attained it, a reference to how settled it is now, and a reference to its age against the chain's tip; the tiers are `INCLUDED`, `SOFT`, `HARD`, and `SETTLED`, ordered, and the references are opaque and orderable
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the attempt rule reads at the reference `current_reference_at` returns for the deployment's declared tier and destroys at the reference it returns for `HARD`, and the age of a reference is the timestamp of the chain's latest block less the timestamp of the reference's block, so the adapter reads the chain and holds no clock (LC-04: "the references at which the attempt rule reads (that tier) and destroys (`HARD`), and reference age for the `τ_soft` bound")
    * `[✅]`   Functional: Base maps `INCLUDED` to the sequencer's latest head, `SOFT` to the safe head posted to L1, `HARD` to the L1-finalized head, and `SETTLED` to the L2 block height of the anchor state of the OP Stack fault-proof registry read from the L1 node the settings name; a reference whose block the chain no longer holds under the identity the reference recorded is refused as reorganized, and a reference past the latest head is refused as not included
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the family's entitlement-state interface evaluates one attempt context, and a page of contexts, against the registry at the context's settlement reference, returning `AUTHORIZED`, `PENDING_SETTLEMENT`, or `DENIED` for each, the batch returning one result per context in the order given and agreeing with the individual evaluation of each context; a context's every field reaches the call
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the attempt context carries the asset's root, the deployment identity, the entitlement contract and token, the identity and the scheme and commitment of its bound handshake key, the interval its credential was issued for, and the settlement reference of the view, generic over the chain family's forms
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the family's estimated-fee description is a list of entries each carrying a name and a unit from the producing member's declaration and a quantity of the family's own type; Base estimates the fee of the transaction its caller describes, without signing, sending, or settling anything, as the execution gas the endpoint estimates and the L1 data fee the gas price oracle returns for the transaction's unsigned encoding, and refuses an L1 data fee that does not fit the family's quantity, a 128-bit unsigned integer, the execution gas converting to it without loss
    * `[✅]`   Functional: recovery returns, for an entitlement and an interval, the envelope bytes the settlement that created the interval posted, taken from that settlement's event and confirmed against the calldata of the transaction that emitted it, and refuses a disagreement between the two or a settlement the history does not hold
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the family's consumer surface hands the constructed chain to a caller generic over the family's trait, the chain's endpoint, chain identifier, and entitlement contract being read from it through the family's trait
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the adapter reads the endpoint, the settlement endpoint, the chain identifier, the registry its views are called on, the entitlement contract, the gas price oracle, the anchor state registry its `SETTLED` tier reads, and the page limit of its batch view from the settings and the declaration `chain/base/settings` authors, and holds none of them as a literal
    * `[✅]`   Functional: `contracts/evm/abi/Registry.json` and `contracts/evm/abi/IEntitlement.json` state, complete, the registry's and the entitlement interface's functions, events, and structures, and the concrete binds them through `alloy`'s `sol!`
    * `[✅]`   Non-functional: adding a further chain concrete is its module, its variant in the member identifier, the selection, and each error union its own errors reach, its entry in the declared set, and its branch in the factory, the selection admission, the declaration lookup, the declared-selection builder, and the composition admission; no consumer changes, and no family surface names Base, an RPC, a gas price oracle, a block tag, or a contract call

  * `[✅]`   `role`
    * `[✅]`   Adapter: the chain family's concrete for Base, private beneath the family's `factory` module, the first concrete that implements the settlement and entitlement-state interfaces, and the first source file that requires them, the attempt context, the family's selection, declared set, consumer surface, fee description, and mock, so this node authors each of them in the `factory` module
    * `[✅]`   Owns the Base client over `alloy`, Base's tier mapping, the registry view calls, the fee estimate, the settlement event and calldata reader, and its own error types; reads the settings type, the declaration constant, and the family's member identifier, endpoint, and declaration types `chain/base/settings` authors
    * `[✅]`   Binds the forms `chain/create_chain_forms` resolves, as the generic parameter of its implementation, and authors no form; address derivation and the signed-intent form are `chain/evm_forms`'
    * `[✅]`   Authors the language-neutral ABI descriptions `contracts/evm/abi/Registry.json` and `contracts/evm/abi/IEntitlement.json` as their first consumer; authors no contract source and no toolchain file
    * `[✅]`   Returns a recovered envelope as the bytes the settlement posted, suite-tagged data the delivery verifier the adapter registry binds interprets, which the envelope family decodes into its own type
    * `[✅]`   Does not sign, send, or settle a transaction, hold a key, submit a signed intent, or read the quorum of several endpoints; the family-owned `chain/quorum_view` aggregates views of several chain adapters, and the submission family submits intents
    * `[✅]`   Does not select a member, build a selection, look up a declaration, or admit a composition; `chain/admit_chain_selection`, `chain/lookup_chain_declaration`, `chain/admit_chain_composition`, and `chain/factory` do, each reaching this concrete through the family's private path
    * `[✅]`   Does not define the settlement tier a deployment declares, the freshness bound, or the attempt rule; the deployment record and the attempt engine do
    * `[✅]`   Does not carry a commit

  * `[✅]`   `module`
    * `[✅]`   Bounded context, in the family's `factory` module of `adapters/chain`, gaining `SettlementTier`, `SettlementReference`, `ReferenceAge`, `AuthorizationResult`, `AttemptContext`, `FeeRequest`, `FeeQuantity`, `FeeEntry`, `FeeDescription`, `RecoveredEnvelope`, `BatchPage`, `ISettlementAdapter`, `IEntitlementStateAdapter`, `IChainAdapter`, `IChainConsumer`, `ChainSelection`, `DECLARED_CHAIN_MEMBERS`, each method's params, payload, success, error, and return types, `MockIChainAdapter` with its failure mode and own errors, and the builders
    * `[✅]`   Bounded context: the private `base` concrete of the `adapters/chain` crate, holding `BaseChain` with its constructor params and error, the sol bindings, the tier mapping, the view calls, the fee estimate, the recovery reader, and the builders; the `settings` module beneath it is `chain/base/settings`'
    * `[✅]`   Edits `adapters/chain/Cargo.toml`, `adapters/chain/src/factory/interface.rs`, `adapters/chain/src/factory/mock.rs`, `adapters/chain/src/base/mod.rs`, and `deny.toml`; creates `adapters/chain/src/base/interface.rs`, `interaction.spec.md`, `mock.rs`, `test.rs`, `integration_test.rs`, and `provides.rs`, `contracts/evm/abi/Registry.json`, and `contracts/evm/abi/IEntitlement.json`
    * `[✅]`   Outside: the family-owned functions and the factory function, the forms' behavior, the quorum of several endpoints, the submission of a transaction, every contract's Solidity realization, the harness's verifier concrete, the daemon's attempt engine, and the hash-card

  * `[✅]`   `deps`
    * `[✅]`   `factory`, same crate, through `crate::factory::provides`: `IChainForms`, `ChainFormsIdentifier`, `ChainFormsDeclaration`, `IIdentityForm`, and `MockIChainForms` with `build_mock_i_chain_forms` and `MockIChainFormsOverrides`, as `chain/evm_forms` authors them; and `CHAIN_INTERFACE_VERSION`, `ChainMemberIdentifier`, `ChainEndpoint`, `FeeEntryDeclaration`, and `ChainDeclaration`, as `chain/base/settings` authors them, with their builders `build_chain_endpoint`, `build_fee_entry_declaration`, and `build_chain_declaration` and their overrides in the tests; this node adds the family items listed under `module` to the same module, which names nothing in `base`
    * `[✅]`   `settings`, `crate::base::settings::provides`, same concrete: `BaseChainSettings`, `BASE_CHAIN_DECLARATION`, and, in the tests, `build_base_chain_settings` with `BaseChainSettingsOverrides`, as `chain/base/settings` authors them; the settings type the constructor params borrow, the declaration constant the adapter's `DECLARATION` names, and the builder every test arranges its settings through
    * `[✅]`   The forms arrive as the type parameter `F` of `BaseChain<F>`, which the family-owned function `chain/create_chain_forms` resolves, its `IChainFormsConsumer` and `ConsumeChainFormsPayload` named only in the integration test through `crate::create_chain_forms::provides`, as `chain/create_chain_forms` authors them

    * `[✅]`   `alloy` `2.5.0`, vendor, named only by `base`: `sol!` over the two ABI descriptions and over an inline `IGasPriceOracle` with `getL1Fee`, an inline `IAnchorStateRegistry` with `getAnchorRoot`; `alloy::primitives::{Address, B256, Bytes, U256, TxKind}`; `alloy::providers::{DynProvider, Provider, ProviderBuilder}`; `alloy::eips::BlockNumberOrTag`; `alloy::network::TransactionBuilder`; `alloy::rpc::types::{Filter, TransactionRequest}`; `alloy::consensus::{SignableTransaction, TxEip1559}`; `alloy::eips::eip2930::AccessList`; `alloy::transports::TransportError`; and, in `base/mock.rs`, `base/test.rs`, and `base/integration_test.rs` only, `alloy::transports::mock::Asserter`
